  FUNCTION: _central_tendency_and_ci_by_prior_and_posterior(prior: jax.Array | None, posterior: jax.Array | None, metric_name: str, xr_dims: collections.abc.Sequence[str], xr_coords: collections.abc.Mapping[str, tuple[collections.abc.Sequence[str], collections.abc.Sequence[str]]], confidence_level: float = 0.9, include_median: bool = False) -> xarray.core.dataset.Dataset
  DOCSTRING: Calculates central tendency and CI of prior/posterior data for a metric.

Args:
  prior: A tensor with the prior data for the metric, or `None` to exclude
    the prior distribution from the output.
  posterior: A tensor with the posterior data for the metric, or `None` to
    exclude the posterior distribution from the output.
  metric_name: The name of the input metric for the computations.
  xr_dims: A list of dimensions for the output dataset.
  xr_coords: A dictionary with the coordinates for the output dataset. The
    `distribution` coordinate must list only the distributions that are not
    `None`, in the order prior, posterior.
  confidence_level: Confidence level for computing credible intervals,
    represented as a value between zero and one.
  include_median: A boolean flag indicating whether to calculate and include
    the median in the output Dataset (default: False).

Returns:
  An xarray Dataset containing central tendency and confidence intervals for
  prior and/or posterior data for the metric.

  FUNCTION: _pct_of_contribution(incremental_outcome: jax.Array | None, expected_outcome: jax.Array | None) -> jax.Array | None
  DOCSTRING: Returns incremental outcome as a % of mean expected outcome.

Args:
  incremental_outcome: Incremental outcome draws, or `None` if the
    distribution is not computed.
  expected_outcome: Expected outcome draws, or `None` if the distribution is
    not computed.

Returns:

  FUNCTION: _warn_if_geo_arg_in_kwargs(**kwargs)

  FUNCTION: get_central_tendency_and_ci(data: numpy.ndarray | jax.Array, confidence_level: float = 0.9, axis: tuple[int, ...] = (0, 1), include_median=False) -> numpy.ndarray
  DOCSTRING: Calculates mean and credible intervals for the given data.

Args:
  data: Data for the metric.
  confidence_level: Confidence level for computing credible intervals,
    represented as a value between zero and one.
  axis: Axis or axes along which the mean, median, and quantiles are computed.
  include_median: A boolean flag indicating whether to calculate and include
    the median in the output Dataset (default: False).

Returns:
  A numpy array or backend.Tensor containing the mean and credible intervals
  for the given data. Optionally, it also includes the median.

  FUNCTION: overrides_incremental_outcome(analyzer_class: type[meridian.analysis.analyzer.Analyzer]) -> bool
  DOCSTRING: Checks whether an analyzer class overrides how incremental outcome works.

Walks the method resolution order of `analyzer_class` up to `Analyzer` and
looks for the `Analyzer` members that determine the incremental outcome of
paid media channels in each class `__dict__`. This doesn't invoke descriptors
such as `backend.function` or `functools.cached_property`. Members of
`Analyzer` itself and attributes set on an instance (for example, by
`mock.patch.object`) aren't considered.

Args:
  analyzer_class: `Analyzer` or a subclass of it.

Returns:
  `True` if a class before `Analyzer` in the method resolution order of
  `analyzer_class` defines a member that determines the incremental outcome,
  and `False` otherwise.

[CLASSES]

  CLASS: History
  SIGNATURE: () -> None
  DOCSTRING:
Uses each time period's own effect.

Each time period uses the effect of the interval it falls in, the same
calculation as `Analyzer.roi()`. Time periods are matched by date, so this is
only defined for time periods in the training data.
  METHODS:
    __delattr__(self, name)
    __eq__(self, other)
    __hash__(self)
    __init__(self) -> None
    __replace__(self, /, **changes)
    __repr__(self)
    __setattr__(self, name, value)

  CLASS: Interval
  SIGNATURE: (*, date: str | datetime.datetime | datetime.date | numpy.datetime64 | None) -> None
  DOCSTRING:
Uses the effect of the interval containing `date` for every time period.

Attributes:
  date: A date in the training data, for example `'2025-03-03'`. For each
    channel with changepoints, the effect of the interval that contains this
    date is used. Channels can have different changepoints, so the date can
    fall in a different interval for each channel. Can be a 'YYYY-MM-DD'
    string, `datetime.date`, `datetime.datetime` or `np.datetime64`, and is
    stored as a `datetime.date`.
  METHODS:
    __delattr__(self, name)
    __eq__(self, other)
    __hash__(self)
    __init__(self, *, date: str | datetime.datetime | datetime.date | numpy.datetime64 | None) -> None
    __post_init__(self)
    __replace__(self, /, **changes)
    __repr__(self)
    __setattr__(self, name, value)

  CLASS: Latest
  SIGNATURE: () -> None
  DOCSTRING:
Uses the effect of each channel's latest interval for every time period.

This assumes that the effect of the latest interval continues, so it can be
used for any time periods, including time periods after the training data.
  METHODS:
    __delattr__(self, name)
    __eq__(self, other)
    __hash__(self)
    __init__(self) -> None
    __replace__(self, /, **changes)
    __repr__(self)
    __setattr__(self, name, value)

[FUNCTIONS]

  FUNCTION: _check_effect(effect: object) -> meridian.analysis.changepoint_effects.Latest | meridian.analysis.changepoint_effects.History | meridian.analysis.changepoint_effects.Interval
  DOCSTRING: Returns `effect` if it's a changepoint effect.

Args:
  effect: The value to check.

Returns:
  `effect`.

Raises:
  TypeError: If `effect` isn't a `Latest`, `History` or `Interval` instance.
    For a class or a string such as `'latest'`, the message suggests the
    right value.

  FUNCTION: _describe(effect: meridian.analysis.changepoint_effects.Latest | meridian.analysis.changepoint_effects.History | meridian.analysis.changepoint_effects.Interval | None) -> str
  DOCSTRING: Writes `effect` the way a user creates it, for messages.

Args:
  effect: The changepoint effect, or `None`.

Returns:
  For example `changepoint_effects.Interval(date='2025-03-03')`, or `None`.

  FUNCTION: _interval_time_index(effect: meridian.analysis.changepoint_effects.Interval, time_coordinates: meridian.data.time_coordinates.TimeCoordinates) -> int
  DOCSTRING: The index of the training time period that contains `effect.date`.

Args:
  effect: The `Interval` effect.
  time_coordinates: The time coordinates of the training data.

Returns:
  The index of the time period that contains the date.

Raises:
  ValueError: If the date is before the first time period or after the last
    one ends.

  FUNCTION: _time_indices(effect: meridian.analysis.changepoint_effects.Latest | meridian.analysis.changepoint_effects.History | meridian.analysis.changepoint_effects.Interval, *, time_coordinates: meridian.data.time_coordinates.TimeCoordinates, dates: collections.abc.Sequence[datetime.date] | None = None) -> numpy.ndarray
  DOCSTRING: For each time period, the training time period whose effect it uses.

Args:
  effect: The changepoint effect.
  time_coordinates: The time coordinates of the training data.
  dates: The time periods to look up, as their start dates. Default: the
    time periods of the training data.

Returns:
  For each date in `dates`, the index of the training time period whose
  effect it uses: the last one for `Latest`, the one that contains the date
  of `Interval`, and the one with the same date for `History`.

Raises:
  ValueError: If the date of an `Interval` isn't in the training data, or
    `History` gets dates that aren't time periods of the training data.

  FUNCTION: _training_range(time_coordinates: meridian.data.time_coordinates.TimeCoordinates) -> tuple[datetime.date, datetime.date]
  DOCSTRING: The first and last days of the training data.

Args:
  time_coordinates: The time coordinates of the training data.

Returns:
  The start of the first time period, and the last day of the last one.

  CLASS: BudgetOptimizer

  CLASS: OptimizationGrid
  SIGNATURE: (_grid_dataset: xarray.core.dataset.Dataset, historical_spend: numpy.ndarray, use_kpi: bool, use_posterior: bool, use_optimal_frequency: bool, start_date: str | datetime.datetime | datetime.date | numpy.datetime64 | None, end_date: str | datetime.datetime | datetime.date | numpy.datetime64 | None, gtol: float, round_factor: int, optimal_frequency: numpy.ndarray | None, selected_geos: collections.abc.Sequence[str] | None, selected_times: collections.abc.Sequence[str] | None, max_frequency: float | None = None, _changepoint_effect: meridian.analysis.changepoint_effects.Latest | meridian.analysis.changepoint_effects.History | meridian.analysis.changepoint_effects.Interval | None = None) -> None
  DOCSTRING:
Optimization grid information.

Attributes:
  historical_spend: ndarray of shape `(n_paid_channels,)` containing
    aggregated historical spend allocation for all media and RF channels.
  use_kpi: Whether using generic KPI or revenue.
  use_posterior: Whether posterior distributions were used, or prior.
  use_optimal_frequency: Whether optimal frequency was used.
  max_frequency: The maximum frequency for reach and frequency channels.
  start_date: The start date of the optimization period.
  end_date: The end date of the optimization period.
  gtol: Float indicating the acceptable relative error for the budget used in
    the grid setup. The budget is rounded by `10^n`, where `n` is the smallest
    integer such that `(budget - rounded_budget)` is less than or equal to
    `(budget * gtol)`.
  round_factor: The round factor used for the optimization grid.
  optimal_frequency: Optional ndarray of shape `(n_paid_channels,)`,
    containing the optimal frequency per channel. Value is `None` if the model
    does not contain reach and frequency data, or if the model does contain
    reach and frequency data, but historical frequency is used for the
    optimization scenario.
  selected_geos: The geo coordinates from the model used in this grid.
  selected_times: The time coordinates from the model used in this grid. This
    is a list of strings indicating the time coordinates used in this grid.
  METHODS:

  CLASS: OptimizationResults
  SIGNATURE: (analyzer: meridian.analysis.analyzer.Analyzer, spend_ratio: numpy.ndarray, spend_bounds: tuple[numpy.ndarray, numpy.ndarray], _nonoptimized_data: xarray.core.dataset.Dataset, _nonoptimized_data_with_optimal_freq: xarray.core.dataset.Dataset, _optimized_data: xarray.core.dataset.Dataset, _optimization_grid: meridian.analysis.optimizer.OptimizationGrid, meridian: meridian.model.model.Meridian | None = None, new_data: meridian.analysis.tensors.DataTensors | None = None) -> None
  DOCSTRING:
The optimized budget allocation.

This is a dataclass object containing datasets output from `BudgetOptimizer`.

The performance metrics (data variables) are: spend, percentage of spend, ROI,
mROI, incremental outcome, CPIK, and effectiveness.

Attributes:
  meridian: The fitted Meridian model that was used to create this budget
    allocation.
  analyzer: The analyzer bound to the model above.
  spend_ratio: The spend ratio used to scale the non-optimized performance
    metrics to the optimized performance metrics.
  spend_bounds: The spend bounds used to scale the non-optimized performance
    metrics to the optimized performance metrics.
  nonoptimized_data: Performance metrics under the non-optimized budget. For
    R&F channels, the non-optimized frequency is used.
  nonoptimized_data_with_optimal_freq: Performance metrics under the
    non-optimized budget. For R&F channels, the optimal frequency is used if
    frequency was optimized.
  optimized_data: Performance metrics under the optimized budget. For R&F
    channels, the optimal frequency is used if frequency was optimized.
  optimization_grid: The grid information used for optimization.
  new_data: The optional `DataTensors` container that was used to create this
    budget allocation.
  METHODS:

  FUNCTION: _exceeds_optimization_constraints(spend: numpy.ndarray, incremental_outcome: numpy.ndarray, roi_grid_point: float, scenario: meridian.analysis.optimizer.FixedBudgetScenario | meridian.analysis.optimizer.FlexibleBudgetScenario) -> bool
  DOCSTRING: Checks optimization scenario constraints.

  Optimality is verified within the optimization constraints, such as budget
  flexibility, target_roi, and target_mroi.

Args:
  spend: np.ndarray with dimensions (`n_total_channels`) containing spend per
    channel for all media and RF channels.
  incremental_outcome: np.ndarray with dimensions (`n_total_channels`)
    containing incremental outcome per channel for all media and RF channels.
  roi_grid_point: float roi for non-optimized optimation step.
  scenario: FixedBudgetScenario or FlexibleBudgetScenario.

Returns:
  bool indicating whether optimal spend and incremental outcome have been
    found, given the optimization constraints.

  FUNCTION: _expand_selected_times(model_context: meridian.model.context.ModelContext, start_date: str | datetime.datetime | datetime.date | numpy.datetime64 | None, end_date: str | datetime.datetime | datetime.date | numpy.datetime64 | None, new_data: meridian.analysis.tensors.DataTensors | None) -> list[str] | None
  DOCSTRING: Creates selected_times from start_date and end_date.

This function creates `selected_times` argument based on `start_date`,
`end_date` and `new_data`. If `new_data` is not used or `new_data.time` is
None, dates are selected from `model_context.expand_selected_time_dims`.
Otherwise, dates are selected from `new_data.expand_selected_time_dims`.

Args:
  model_context: The `ModelContext` object with original data.
  start_date: Start date of the selected time period.
  end_date: End date of the selected time period.
  new_data: The optional `DataTensors` object.

Returns:
  If both `start_date` and `end_date` are `None`, returns `None`. Otherwise,
  returns a list of strings with selected dates.

  FUNCTION: _expand_tensor(tensor: jax.Array, required_shape: tuple[int, ...])
  DOCSTRING: Expands a tensor to the required number of dimensions.

  FUNCTION: _get_spend_bounds(n_channels: int, spend_constraint_lower: float | collections.abc.Sequence[float], spend_constraint_upper: float | collections.abc.Sequence[float]) -> tuple[numpy.ndarray, numpy.ndarray]
  DOCSTRING: Get spend bounds from spend constraints.

Args:
  n_channels: Integer number of total channels.
  spend_constraint_lower: Numeric list of size `n_total_channels` or float
    (same constraint for all media) indicating the lower bound of media-level
    spend. The lower bound of media-level spend is `(1 -
    spend_constraint_lower) * budget * allocation`. The value must be between
    0 and 1.
  spend_constraint_upper: Numeric list of size `n_total_channels` or float
    (same constraint for all media) indicating the upper bound of media-level
    spend. The upper bound of media-level spend is `(1 +
    spend_constraint_upper) * budget * allocation`.

Returns:
  spend_bounds: tuple of np.ndarray of size `n_total_channels` containing
    the untreated lower and upper bound spend for each media and RF channel.

  FUNCTION: _raise_warning_if_response_curves_non_monotonic(incremental_outcome_grid: xarray.core.dataarray.DataArray, channels: collections.abc.Sequence[str]) -> None
  DOCSTRING: Raises a warning if the response curve of any channel is non-monotonic.

Response curves can be non-monotonic (that is, have regions of negative slope)
when `media_effects_dist='normal'`, because some geos may have negative
coefficient estimates and geos saturate at different rates. The hill-climbing
optimization algorithm assumes concave response curves and may not find the
global optimum when a response curve is non-monotonic.

Args:
  incremental_outcome_grid: Discrete grid with dimensions (`grid_length` x
    `n_channels`) containing incremental outcome by channel, where the spend
    is increasing along the first dimension. Missing grid points are `NaN`.
  channels: The channel names, in the same order as the grid columns.

  FUNCTION: _raise_warning_if_target_constraints_not_met(target_roi: float | None, target_mroi: float | None, optimized_data: xarray.core.dataset.Dataset) -> None
  DOCSTRING: Raises a warning if the target constraints are not met.

  FUNCTION: _validate_budget(fixed_budget: bool, budget: float | None, target_roi: float | None, target_mroi: float | None) -> None
  DOCSTRING: Validates the budget optimization arguments.

  FUNCTION: _validate_pct_of_spend(n_channels: int, hist_spend: numpy.ndarray, pct_of_spend: collections.abc.Sequence[float] | None) -> numpy.ndarray
  DOCSTRING: Validates and returns the percent of spend.

  FUNCTION: _validate_spend_constraints(n_channels: int, const_lower: float | collections.abc.Sequence[float], const_upper: float | collections.abc.Sequence[float]) -> tuple[numpy.ndarray, numpy.ndarray]
  DOCSTRING: Validates and returns the spend constraint requirements.

  FUNCTION: _with_changepoint_effect(method: ~_Method) -> ~_Method
  DOCSTRING: Runs a `BudgetOptimizer` method with a changepoint effect.

The method's `_changepoint_effect` argument is resolved by
`BudgetOptimizer._use_changepoint_effect`, and every outcome that the method
computes uses that effect for the channels with changepoints.

Args:
  method: A `BudgetOptimizer` method with a keyword-only
    `_changepoint_effect` argument.

Returns:
  The method, with its `_changepoint_effect` resolved.

  FUNCTION: get_optimization_bounds(n_channels: int, spend: numpy.ndarray, round_factor: int, spend_constraint_lower: float | collections.abc.Sequence[float], spend_constraint_upper: float | collections.abc.Sequence[float]) -> tuple[numpy.ndarray, numpy.ndarray]
  DOCSTRING: Get optimization bounds from spend and spend constraints.

Args:
  n_channels: Integer number of total channels.
  spend: np.ndarray with size `n_total_channels` containing media-level spend
    for all media and RF channels.
  round_factor: Integer number of digits to round optimization bounds to.
  spend_constraint_lower: Numeric list of size `n_total_channels` or float
    (same constraint for all media) indicating the lower bound of media-level
    spend. The lower bound of media-level spend is `(1 -
    spend_constraint_lower) * budget * allocation`. The value must be between
    0 and 1.
  spend_constraint_upper: Numeric list of size `n_total_channels` or float
    (same constraint for all media) indicating the upper bound of media-level
    spend. The upper bound of media-level spend is `(1 +
    spend_constraint_upper) * budget * allocation`.

Returns:
  lower_bound: np.ndarray of size `n_total_channels` containing the treated
    lower bound spend for each media and RF channel.
  upper_bound: np.ndarray of size `n_total_channels` containing the treated
    upper bound spend for each media and RF channel.

  FUNCTION: get_round_factor(budget: float, gtol: float) -> int
  DOCSTRING: Gets the number of integer digits to round off of budget.

Args:
  budget: Float number for total advertising budget.
  gtol: Float indicating the acceptable relative error for the budget used in
    the grid setup. The budget will be rounded by `10^n`, where `n` is the
    smallest int such that `(budget - rounded_budget) <= (budget * gtol)`.
    `gtol` must be less than 1.

Returns:
  Integer number of digits to round budget to.

[FUNCTIONS]

  FUNCTION: build_calibration_overview_chart(calibrated_output: meridian.model.calibration.base.CalibrationOutput | None, calibrated_prior_dist: tensorflow_probability.substrates.jax.distributions.distribution.Distribution | None, posterior_samples: numpy.ndarray | None = None, *, limit_experiments: int | None = 5, sort_experiments: bool = True) -> altair.vegalite.v5.api.HConcatChart | None
  DOCSTRING: Builds the 1x3 side-by-side calibration overview chart for a channel.

Args:
  calibrated_output: The calibration output for the channel.
  calibrated_prior_dist: The 1D calibrated prior distribution for the channel.
  posterior_samples: Optional 1D array of posterior ROI samples. If None or
    empty, the right subplot shows the intermediary prior instead.
  limit_experiments: Optional maximum number of experiments to display. If
    None, all experiments are displayed.
  sort_experiments: If True, ranks experiments by adjusted standard error
    (ascending) before applying `limit_experiments`.

Returns:
  The chart, or None if the calibration output or the calibrated prior is
  missing.

  CLASS: BayesianPPPCheck
  SIGNATURE: (*, meridian: typing.Any | None = None, model_context: meridian.model.context.ModelContext | None = None, inference_data: arviz.data.inference_data.InferenceData | None = None, analyzer: meridian.analysis.analyzer.Analyzer, config: ~ConfigType, selected_geos: collections.abc.Sequence[str] | None = None, selected_times: collections.abc.Sequence[str] | None = None)
  DOCSTRING:
Checks the Bayesian posterior predictive p-value of the total outcome.

The test statistic is the outcome summed over the selected geos and times.
For each posterior draw, the model's predictive distribution of this total is
normal, with mean equal to the expected total outcome and variance equal to
the summed residual variance of the selected geo and time cells. The check
computes the two-sided posterior predictive p-value in closed form: the
average, over posterior draws, of the probability that a predictive total
lands at least as far from the posterior predictive mean as the observed
total. The check fails if the p-value is below `ppp_threshold`.
  METHODS:
    __init__(self, *, meridian: typing.Any | None = None, model_context: meridian.model.context.ModelContext | None = None, inference_data: arviz.data.inference_data.InferenceData | None = None, analyzer: meridian.analysis.analyzer.Analyzer, config: ~ConfigType, selected_geos: collections.abc.Sequence[str] | None = None, selected_times: collections.abc.Sequence[str] | None = None)
    _calculate_total_sigma(self) -> numpy.ndarray
    run(self) -> meridian.analysis.review.results.BayesianPPPCheckResult

  CLASS: _ROIConsistencyChannelData
  SIGNATURE: (prior_roi_los: numpy.ndarray, prior_roi_his: numpy.ndarray, posterior_means: numpy.ndarray, all_channels: numpy.ndarray, inf_channels: numpy.ndarray, low_roi_channels: numpy.ndarray, high_roi_channels: numpy.ndarray, quantile_not_defined_channels: numpy.ndarray, quantile_not_defined_parameters: list[tensorflow_probability.substrates.jax.distributions.distribution.Distribution] = <factory>) -> None
  DOCSTRING:
A data structure for auxiliary data for the ROI Consistency Check.

Attributes:
  prior_roi_los: Lower quantile values from ROI priors, corresponding to the
    channels in `all_channels`.
  prior_roi_his: Upper quantile values from ROI priors, corresponding to the
    channels in `all_channels`.
  posterior_means: Mean values of ROI posteriors, corresponding to the
    channels in `all_channels`.
  all_channels: Channel names for which quantile computations were successful;
    channels for which quantiles could not be computed are skipped. They are
    ordered with media channels (`roi_m`) followed by reach and frequency (RF)
    channels (`roi_rf`).
  inf_channels: Channels with infinite prior quantiles.
  low_roi_channels: Channels with posterior means below their prior's lower
    quantile.
  high_roi_channels: Channels with posterior means above their prior's upper
    quantile.
  quantile_not_defined_channels: Channel names for which quantiles could not
    be computed.
  quantile_not_defined_parameters: Parameters for which the quantile method is
    not implemented.
  METHODS:
    __eq__(self, other)
    __init__(self, prior_roi_los: numpy.ndarray, prior_roi_his: numpy.ndarray, posterior_means: numpy.ndarray, all_channels: numpy.ndarray, inf_channels: numpy.ndarray, low_roi_channels: numpy.ndarray, high_roi_channels: numpy.ndarray, quantile_not_defined_channels: numpy.ndarray, quantile_not_defined_parameters: list[tensorflow_probability.substrates.jax.distributions.distribution.Distribution] = <factory>) -> None
    __replace__(self, /, **changes)

  FUNCTION: _bootstrap(x: numpy.ndarray, n_bootstraps: int, n_samples_per_bootstrap: int | None = None) -> numpy.ndarray
  DOCSTRING: Performs non-parametric bootstrap resampling on the columns of x.

Args:
  x: A 2D array of shape `(n_channels, n_posterior_samples)` containing the
    posterior samples per channel.
  n_bootstraps: Number of bootstrap samples to use for calculating posterior
    statistics.
  n_samples_per_bootstrap: Number of posterior samples to draw with
    replacement for each bootstrap iteration. If None, uses all available
    posterior samples.

Returns:
  A 3D array of shape `(n_bootstraps, n_channels, n_samples_per_bootstrap)`
  with the resampled data.

  FUNCTION: _calculate_new_statistics_from_samples(inference_data: arviz.data.inference_data.InferenceData, n_bootstraps: int, var_name: str, n_channels: int, n_samples_per_bootstrap: int | None = None) -> dict[str, numpy.ndarray]
  DOCSTRING: Calculate Mean, Median, Q1, and Q3 from posterior samples.

Args:

  FUNCTION: _set_metrics_from_gof_dataframe(metrics: collections.abc.MutableMapping[str, float], gof_df: pandas.core.frame.DataFrame, geo_granularity: str, suffix: str) -> None
  DOCSTRING: Sets the `metrics` variable of the GoodnessOfFitCheckResult.

This method takes a DataFrame containing goodness of fit metrics and pivots it
to a Series, which is then added to the `metrics` variable of the
`GoodnessOfFitCheckResult`.

Args:
  metrics: A dictionary to store the goodness of fit metrics in.
  gof_df: A DataFrame containing predictive accuracy of the whole data (if
    holdout set is not used) of filtered to a single evaluation set ("all",
    "train", or "test").
  geo_granularity: The geo granularity of the data ("geo" or "national").
  suffix: A suffix to add to the metric names (e.g., "_train", "_test").

  FUNCTION: _two_sided_normal_mixture_p_value(observed: float, means: numpy.ndarray, stddevs: numpy.ndarray) -> float
  DOCSTRING: Computes a two-sided p-value under an equal-weight mixture of normals.

Each mixture component corresponds to one posterior draw `s` and is normal
with mean `means[s]` and standard deviation `stddevs[s]`. With
`center = mean(means)` and `distance = |observed - center|`, the p-value is
the probability that a draw from the mixture lands at least `distance` away
from `center`:

  mean_s(Phi((center - distance - means[s]) / stddevs[s])
         + Phi((means[s] - center - distance) / stddevs[s])),

where `Phi` is the standard normal CDF. Computing this probability exactly,
rather than estimating it from random draws, makes the result deterministic.
A component with a zero standard deviation is a point mass, and contributes 1
if `|means[s] - center| >= distance` and 0 otherwise. A NaN standard deviation
makes the result NaN.

Args:
  observed: The observed value of the test statistic.
  means: The mean of each mixture component.
  stddevs: The non-negative standard deviation of each mixture component, with
    the same shape as `means`.

Returns:
  The two-sided p-value.

Raises:
  ValueError: If `means` and `stddevs` have different shapes.

  CLASS: BaselineConfig
  SIGNATURE: (negative_baseline_prob_review_threshold: float = 0.2, negative_baseline_prob_fail_threshold: float = 0.8) -> None
  DOCSTRING:
Configuration for the Baseline Check.

This check warns if there is a high probability of a negative baseline.

Attributes:
  negative_baseline_prob_review_threshold: Probability threshold for a review.
    If the probability of a negative baseline is above this value, a review is
    issued.
  negative_baseline_prob_fail_threshold: Probability threshold for a failure.
    If the probability of a negative baseline is above this value, the check
    fails.
  METHODS:

    __eq__(self, other)

  CLASS: ChannelCheckConfig
  SIGNATURE: (failing_channels_threshold: int | None = None, failing_channels_ratio_threshold: float | None = None) -> None
  DOCSTRING:
Base configuration class for channel-level health checks.

If both `failing_channels_threshold` and `failing_channels_ratio_threshold`
are set, the check passes if EITHER condition is satisfied (or both).

Attributes:
  failing_channels_threshold: Absolute threshold for the number of failing
    channels allowed before triggering a review status. If None, absolute
    count tolerance is not applied. If both failing_channels_threshold and
    failing_channels_ratio_threshold are specified, the check passes if EITHER
    condition is satisfied (or both).
  failing_channels_ratio_threshold: The maximum ratio (between 0.0 and 1.0) of
    failing channels allowed before triggering a review status. If None, ratio
    tolerance is not applied. If both failing_channels_threshold and
    failing_channels_ratio_threshold are specified, the check passes if EITHER
    condition is satisfied (or both).
  METHODS:
    __delattr__(self, name)
    __eq__(self, other)
    __hash__(self)
    __init__(self, failing_channels_threshold: int | None = None, failing_channels_ratio_threshold: float | None = None) -> None
    __post_init__(self)
    __replace__(self, /, **changes)
    __repr__(self)
    __setattr__(self, name, value)
    is_failing_channels_within_threshold(self, n_failing: int, n_total: int) -> bool

  CLASS: ConvergenceConfig
  SIGNATURE: (convergence_threshold: float = 1.2, not_fully_converged_threshold: float = 10.0) -> None
  DOCSTRING:
Configuration for the Convergence Check.

Attributes:
  convergence_threshold: The threshold for the R-hat statistic to determine if
    the model has converged. R-hat values below this are considered converged.
  not_fully_converged_threshold: The threshold for the R-hat statistic to
    determine if the model is not fully converged but potentially acceptable.
    R-hat values between `convergence_threshold` and this value are considered
    not fully converged. R-hat values above this threshold are considered not
    converged.
  METHODS:
    __delattr__(self, name)
    __eq__(self, other)
    __hash__(self)
    __init__(self, convergence_threshold: float = 1.2, not_fully_converged_threshold: float = 10.0) -> None
    __replace__(self, /, **changes)
    __repr__(self)
    __setattr__(self, name, value)

  DOCSTRING:
Configuration for the Prior-Posterior Shift Check.

Attributes:

    statistics.

  CLASS: HighVarianceChannelResult
  SIGNATURE: (case: meridian.analysis.review.results.HighVarianceChannelCases, channel_name: str, spend_share: float, relative_width_ratio: float) -> None
  DOCSTRING:
The immutable result of High Variance Check for a single channel.

Attributes:
  case: The specific case for this channel's high variance check.
  spend_share: The proportion of total spend for this channel.
  relative_width_ratio: The ratio of the posterior ROI credible interval width
    to the prior width.
  METHODS:
    __delattr__(self, name)

  CLASS: Status
  SIGNATURE: (*values)
  DOCSTRING:
Create a collection of name/value pairs.

Example enumeration:

>>> class Color(Enum):
...     RED = 1
...     BLUE = 2
...     GREEN = 3

Access them by:

- attribute access:

  >>> Color.RED
  <Color.RED: 1>

- value lookup:

  >>> Color(1)
  <Color.RED: 1>

- name lookup:

  >>> Color['RED']
  <Color.RED: 1>

Enumerations can be iterated over, and know how many members they have:

>>> len(Color)
3

>>> list(Color)
[<Color.RED: 1>, <Color.BLUE: 2>, <Color.GREEN: 3>]

Methods can be added to enumerations, and members can have their own
attributes -- see the documentation for details.

  CLASS: ModelReviewer
  SIGNATURE: (meridian: typing.Any | None = None, model_context: meridian.model.context.ModelContext | None = None, inference_data: arviz.data.inference_data.InferenceData | None = None, convergence_check_config: meridian.analysis.review.configs.ConvergenceConfig | None = None, post_convergence_checks: Optional[Mapping[Type[meridian.analysis.review.checks.BaseCheck], meridian.analysis.review.configs.BaseConfig]] = None)
  DOCSTRING:
A tool for executing a series of quality checks on a Meridian model.

The reviewer first runs a convergence check. If the model has converged, it
proceeds to run a battery of post-convergence checks.

The battery of post-convergence checks includes:
  - BaselineCheck
  - BayesianPPPCheck
  - GoodnessOfFitCheck
  - PriorPosteriorShiftCheck
  - ROIConsistencyCheck
  METHODS:
    __init__(self, meridian: typing.Any | None = None, model_context: meridian.model.context.ModelContext | None = None, inference_data: arviz.data.inference_data.InferenceData | None = None, convergence_check_config: meridian.analysis.review.configs.ConvergenceConfig | None = None, post_convergence_checks: Optional[Mapping[Type[meridian.analysis.review.checks.BaseCheck], meridian.analysis.review.configs.BaseConfig]] = None)

  CLASS: DataTensors
  SIGNATURE: (media: jax.Array | None = None, media_spend: jax.Array | None = None, reach: jax.Array | None = None, frequency: jax.Array | None = None, rf_impressions: jax.Array | None = None, rf_spend: jax.Array | None = None, organic_media: jax.Array | None = None, organic_reach: jax.Array | None = None, organic_frequency: jax.Array | None = None, non_media_treatments: jax.Array | None = None, controls: jax.Array | None = None, revenue_per_kpi: jax.Array | None = None, time: collections.abc.Sequence[str] | jax.Array | None = None)
  DOCSTRING:
Container for data variable arguments of Analyzer methods.

Attributes:
  media: Optional tensor with dimensions `(n_geos, T, n_media_channels)` for
    any time dimension `T`.
  media_spend: Optional tensor with dimensions `(n_media_channels,)` or
    `(n_geos, T, n_media_channels)` for any time dimension `T`. If the object
    includes variables with modified time periods, then this tensor must be
    provided at the geo and time granularity. Spend provided with dimensions
    `(n_media_channels,)` is allocated across geos and times proportionally
    to `media` before it is used in any analysis.
  reach: Optional tensor with dimensions `(n_geos, T, n_rf_channels)` for any
    time dimension `T`.
  frequency: Optional tensor with dimensions `(n_geos, T, n_rf_channels)` for
    any time dimension `T`.
  rf_impressions: Optional tensor with dimensions `(n_geos, T, n_rf_channels)`
    for any time dimension `T`.
  rf_spend: Optional tensor with dimensions `(n_rf_channels,)` or `(n_geos, T,
    n_rf_channels)` for any time dimension `T`. If the object includes
    variables with modified time periods, then this tensor must be provided at
    the geo and time granularity. Spend provided with dimensions
    `(n_rf_channels,)` is allocated across geos and times proportionally to
    `reach * frequency` before it is used in any analysis.
  organic_media: Optional tensor with dimensions `(n_geos, T,
    n_organic_media_channels)` for any time dimension `T`.
  organic_reach: Optional tensor with dimensions `(n_geos, T,
    n_organic_rf_channels)` for any time dimension `T`.
  organic_frequency: Optional tensor with dimensions `(n_geos, T,
    n_organic_rf_channels)` for any time dimension `T`.
  non_media_treatments: Optional tensor with dimensions `(n_geos, T,
    n_non_media_channels)` for any time dimension `T`.
  controls: Optional tensor with dimensions `(n_geos, n_times, n_controls)`.
  revenue_per_kpi: Optional tensor with dimensions `(n_geos, T)` for any time
    dimension `T`.
  time: Optional sequence or array of date coordinates (strings in
    "YYYY-MM-DD" format, `Date` objects, or datetime objects) corresponding to
    time dimension `T`. Required if any tensor has a modified time dimension
    `T` differing from historical model dimensions. If omitted and tensor
    shapes match the original model dimensions, `time` is automatically
    populated from the model's historical time coordinates.
  METHODS:
    __eq__(self, other: Any) -> bool
    __init__(self, media: jax.Array | None = None, media_spend: jax.Array | None = None, reach: jax.Array | None = None, frequency: jax.Array | None = None, rf_impressions: jax.Array | None = None, rf_spend: jax.Array | None = None, organic_media: jax.Array | None = None, organic_reach: jax.Array | None = None, organic_frequency: jax.Array | None = None, non_media_treatments: jax.Array | None = None, controls: jax.Array | None = None, revenue_per_kpi: jax.Array | None = None, time: collections.abc.Sequence[str] | jax.Array | None = None)
    __replace__(self, /, **changes)
    __repr__(self)
    _tree_flatten(self)
    _validate_n_dims(self)
    expand_selected_time_dims(self, start_date: str | datetime.datetime | datetime.date | numpy.datetime64 | None = None, end_date: str | datetime.datetime | datetime.date | numpy.datetime64 | None = None) -> list[str] | None
    filter_fields(self, fields: collections.abc.Sequence[str]) -> Self

  FUNCTION: _check_n_dims(tensor: jax.Array, name: str, n_dims: int) -> None
  DOCSTRING: Raises an error if the tensor has the wrong number of dimensions.

  FUNCTION: _scale_tensors_by_multiplier(data: meridian.analysis.tensors.DataTensors, multiplier: float, by_reach: bool) -> meridian.analysis.tensors.DataTensors
  DOCSTRING: Gets scaled tensors for incremental outcome calculation.

Args:
  data: DataTensors object containing the optional tensors to scale. Only
    `media`, `reach`, `frequency`, `organic_media`, `organic_reach`, and
    `organic_frequency` are scaled. The other tensors remain unchanged.
  multiplier: Float indicating the factor to scale tensors by.
  by_reach: Boolean indicating whether to scale reach or frequency when rf
    data is available.

Returns:
  A `DataTensors` object containing scaled tensor parameters. The original
  tensors that should not be scaled remain unchanged.

[CLASSES]

  CLASS: WeeklyOptimizationGrid
  SIGNATURE: (incremental_outcome: xarray.core.dataarray.DataArray | None, rf_incremental_outcome: xarray.core.dataarray.DataArray | None, nonoptimized_spend: xarray.core.dataarray.DataArray, use_kpi: bool, use_posterior: bool, multiplier_step: float, max_budget_percent_decrease: float, max_budget_percent_increase: float, max_constraint_variation: float, use_optimal_frequency: bool = True, max_frequency: float | None = None) -> None
  DOCSTRING:
Weekly optimization grid information.

Attributes:
  incremental_outcome: xr.DataArray of shape `(n_media_channels,
    n_spend_multipliers, n_times)` containing incremental outcome for all
    impression-based paid media channels. `None` if the model has no media
    channels.
  rf_incremental_outcome: xr.DataArray of shape `(n_rf_channels,
    n_frequencies, n_times)` containing the incremental outcome of every
    reach and frequency channel at historical spend (spend multiplier `1.0`)
    for each candidate frequency. Impressions are held fixed as frequency
    varies, so reach is `impressions / frequency`. Since the RF incremental
    outcome is linear in reach (and therefore spend) for a fixed frequency,
    the outcome at any spend multiplier `M` is `M` times this value. The
    optimal frequency for an optimization period is resolved at
    `to_optimization_grid` time by maximizing the outcome summed over the
    selected weeks. If `use_optimal_frequency` is `False`, the frequency
    dimension has a single `NaN` coordinate and holds the outcome at
    historical frequency. `None` if the model has no RF channels.
  nonoptimized_spend: xr.DataArray of shape `(n_paid_channels, n_times)`
    containing non-aggregated spend allocation for all paid channels.
  use_kpi: Whether using generic KPI or revenue.
  use_posterior: Whether posterior distributions were used, or prior.
  multiplier_step: Multiplier step size for the spend multiplier.
  max_budget_percent_decrease: Maximum percentage decrease in budget allowed.
  max_budget_percent_increase: Maximum percentage increase in budget allowed.
  max_constraint_variation: Maximum constraint variation allowed.
  use_optimal_frequency: Whether optimal frequency was used.
  max_frequency: Maximum frequency value used for optimal frequency.
  METHODS:
    __delattr__(self, name)
    __eq__(self, other)
    __hash__(self)
    __init__(self, incremental_outcome: xarray.core.dataarray.DataArray | None, rf_incremental_outcome: xarray.core.dataarray.DataArray | None, nonoptimized_spend: xarray.core.dataarray.DataArray, use_kpi: bool, use_posterior: bool, multiplier_step: float, max_budget_percent_decrease: float, max_budget_percent_increase: float, max_constraint_variation: float, use_optimal_frequency: bool = True, max_frequency: float | None = None) -> None
    __replace__(self, /, **changes)
    __repr__(self)
    __setattr__(self, name, value)
    _aggregate_incremental_outcome(effect_diff: jax.Array, beta: jax.Array, revenue_per_kpi: jax.Array, time_indices: jax.Array | None, population: jax.Array, kpi_transformer: Any, use_kpi: bool) -> jax.Array
    _create_grids(self, *, spend_bound_lower: numpy.ndarray, spend_bound_upper: numpy.ndarray, step_size: int, spend: numpy.ndarray | None = None, selected_times: collections.abc.Sequence[str] | None = None) -> tuple[numpy.ndarray, numpy.ndarray]
    _resolve_rf_outcomes(self, selected_times: collections.abc.Sequence[str]) -> tuple[numpy.ndarray | None, numpy.ndarray]
    _validate_dates(self, *, start_date: str | datetime.datetime | datetime.date | numpy.datetime64 | None = None, end_date: str | datetime.datetime | datetime.date | numpy.datetime64 | None = None) -> bool
    _validate_optimization_bounds(self, *, lower_bound: numpy.ndarray, upper_bound: numpy.ndarray, hist_spend: numpy.ndarray, round_factor: int) -> bool
    to_optimization_grid(self, start_date: str | datetime.datetime | datetime.date | numpy.datetime64 | None = None, end_date: str | datetime.datetime | datetime.date | numpy.datetime64 | None = None, budget: float | None = None, spend_constraint_lower: float | collections.abc.Sequence[float] | None = None, spend_constraint_upper: float | collections.abc.Sequence[float] | None = None) -> meridian.analysis.optimizer.OptimizationGrid | None

  FUNCTION: TensorShape(dims)
  DOCSTRING: JAX implementation for TensorShape.

  FUNCTION: _check_scalar_output(value: Any, fn_name: str) -> None
  DOCSTRING: Raises `ValueError` if `value` is not a scalar tensor.

  FUNCTION: _convert_to_tensor(data, dtype=None)
  DOCSTRING: Converts data to a JAX array, handling strings as NumPy arrays.

This function explicitly unwraps objects with a `.values` attribute (e.g.,
pandas.DataFrame, xarray.DataArray) to access the underlying NumPy array,
provided that `.values` is not a method. This takes precedence over the
`__array__` protocol.

It also handles precision mismatches: if `data` is float64 and `dtype` is
not specified, and JAX x64 mode is disabled (default), it issues a warning
and explicitly casts to float32 to match the backend default and prevent
silent precision loss or type errors in downstream operations.

Args:
  data: The data to convert.
  dtype: The desired data type.

Returns:
  A JAX array, or a NumPy array if the dtype is a string type.

  FUNCTION: _extract_int_seed(s: Any) -> Optional[int]
  DOCSTRING: Attempts to extract a scalar Python integer from various input types.

Args:
  s: The input seed, which can be an int, Tensor, or array-like object.

Returns:
  A Python integer if a scalar integer can be extracted, otherwise None.

  FUNCTION: _jax_adstock_process(media: '_jax.Array', weights: '_jax.Array', n_times_output: int) -> '_jax.Array'
  DOCSTRING: Dispatches adstock_process to the appropriate implementation.

  FUNCTION: _jax_adstock_process_conv(media: '_jax.Array', weights: '_jax.Array', n_times_output: int) -> '_jax.Array'
  DOCSTRING: JAX implementation for adstock_process using convolution (GPU optimized).

This function applies an adstock process to media spend data using a
convolutional approach. The weights represent the adstock decay over time.

Args:
  media: A JAX array of media spend. Expected shape is `(batch_dims, n_geos,
    n_times_in, n_channels)`.
  weights: A JAX array of adstock weights. Expected shape is `(batch_dims,
    n_channels, window_size)`, where `batch_dims` must be broadcastable to
    the batch dimensions of `media`.
  n_times_output: The number of time periods in the output. This corresponds
    to `n_times_in - window_size + 1`.

Returns:
  A JAX array representing the adstocked media, with shape
  `(batch_dims, n_geos, n_times_output, n_channels)`.

  FUNCTION: _jax_adstock_process_cpu(media: '_jax.Array', weights: '_jax.Array', n_times_output: int) -> '_jax.Array'
  DOCSTRING: JAX implementation for adstock_process using stack/einsum (CPU optimized).

This mirrors the TensorFlow implementation logic to avoid the memory
overhead of lowering convolution to matrix multiplication on CPU.

Args:
  media: A JAX array of media spend.
  weights: A JAX array of adstock weights.
  n_times_output: The number of time periods in the output.

Returns:
  A JAX array representing the adstocked media.

  FUNCTION: _jax_arange(start: Any, stop: Optional[Any] = None, step: Any = 1, dtype: Optional[Any] = None) -> '_jax.Array'
  DOCSTRING: JAX implementation for arange.

  FUNCTION: _jax_argmax(tensor, axis=None)
  DOCSTRING: JAX implementation for argmax, aligned with TensorFlow's default.

This function finds the indices of the maximum values along a specified axis.
Crucially, it mimics the default behavior of TensorFlow's `tf.argmax`, where
if `axis` is `None`, the operation defaults to `axis=0`. This differs from
NumPy's and JAX's native `argmax` behavior, which would flatten the array
before finding the index.

Args:
  tensor: The input JAX array.
  axis: An integer specifying the axis along which to find the index of the
    maximum value. If `None`, it defaults to `0` to match TensorFlow's
    behavior.

Returns:
  A JAX array containing the indices of the maximum values.

  FUNCTION: _jax_boolean_mask(tensor, mask, axis=None)
  DOCSTRING: JAX implementation for boolean_mask that supports an axis argument.

  FUNCTION: _jax_broadcast_dynamic_shape(shape_x, shape_y)
  DOCSTRING: JAX implementation for broadcast_dynamic_shape.

  FUNCTION: _jax_cast(x: Any, dtype: Any) -> '_jax.Array'
  DOCSTRING: JAX implementation for cast.

  FUNCTION: _jax_cholesky_solve(chol: Any, rhs: Any) -> '_jax.Array'
  DOCSTRING: Solves `(chol @ chol.T) @ x = rhs` given a lower-triangular Cholesky factor.

Args:
  chol: Lower-triangular Cholesky factor of shape `(..., n, n)`.
  rhs: Right-hand side tensor. Treated as a vector (or batch of vectors) of
    shape `(..., n)` when `len(rhs.shape) == len(chol.shape) - 1`, or as a
    matrix (or batch of matrices) of shape `(..., n, k)` when `rhs` has the
    same rank as `chol`.

Returns:
  A JAX array with the same shape as `rhs` containing the solution `x`.

  FUNCTION: _jax_compile_temporary_function(func: collections.abc.Callable[..., typing.Any]) -> collections.abc.Callable[..., typing.Any]
  DOCSTRING: Compiles a function that its caller creates anew on every call.

Args:
  func: The function to compile.

Returns:
  `func` compiled with `jax.jit`.

  FUNCTION: _jax_convert_to_tensor(data, dtype=None)
  DOCSTRING: Converts data to a JAX array, handling strings as NumPy arrays.

This function explicitly unwraps objects with a `.values` attribute (e.g.,
pandas.DataFrame, xarray.DataArray) to access the underlying NumPy array,
provided that `.values` is not a method. This takes precedence over the
`__array__` protocol.

It also handles precision mismatches: if `data` is float64 and `dtype` is
not specified, and JAX x64 mode is disabled (default), it issues a warning
and explicitly casts to float32 to match the backend default and prevent
silent precision loss or type errors in downstream operations.

Args:
  data: The data to convert.
  dtype: The desired data type.

Returns:
  A JAX array, or a NumPy array if the dtype is a string type.

  FUNCTION: _jax_divide_no_nan(x, y)

  FUNCTION: _jax_function_wrapper(func=None, **kwargs)
  DOCSTRING: A wrapper for jax.jit that handles TF-like args and static args.

This wrapper provides compatibility with TensorFlow's `tf.function` arguments
and improves ergonomics when decorating class methods in JAX.

By default, if neither `static_argnums` nor `static_argnames` are provided, it
defaults `static_argnums` to `(0,)`. This assumes the function is a method
where the first argument (`self` or `cls`) should be treated as static.

To disable this behavior for plain functions, explicitly provide an empty
tuple: `@backend.function(static_argnums=())`.

Args:
  func: The function to wrap.
  **kwargs: Keyword arguments passed to jax.jit. TF-specific arguments (like
    `jit_compile`) are ignored.

Returns:
  The wrapped function or a decorator.

  FUNCTION: result_type(*types: Any) -> str
  DOCSTRING: Infers the result dtype from a list of input types, backend-agnostically.

This acts as the single source of truth for type promotion rules. The
promotion logic is designed to be consistent across all backends.

Rule: If any input is a float, the result is float32. Otherwise, the result
is int64 to match NumPy/JAX's default behavior for precision.

Args:
  *types: A variable number of type objects (e.g., `<class 'int'>`,
    np.dtype('float32')).

Returns:
  A string representing the promoted dtype.

  FUNCTION: xla_windowed_adaptive_nuts(*, step_callback: Optional[collections.abc.Callable[[int], None]] = None, **kwargs)
  DOCSTRING: Runs `tfp.experimental.mcmc.windowed_adaptive_nuts` compiled with XLA.

Args:
  step_callback: Optional callable invoked on the host after every MCMC
    step, adaptation steps included, with the number of chains whose
    transition diverged. It runs on a JAX callback thread and must be fast;
    exceptions it raises are reported as warnings. Reporting steps holds the
    adaptation draws in device memory until the run finishes, which raises
    the draw-buffer memory by `num_adaptation_steps / n_draws`. Reporting
    steps relies on the default trace function, so it cannot be combined
    with a custom `trace_fn`.
  **kwargs: Arguments of `tfp.experimental.mcmc.windowed_adaptive_nuts`.

Returns:
  The sampler output, as `tfp.experimental.mcmc.windowed_adaptive_nuts`
  returns it.

Raises:
  ValueError: If both `step_callback` and `trace_fn` are given.

['Backend', 'ComputationBackend', 'ComputationPrecision', 'TENSORFLOW_DEPRECATION_WARNING', 'Union', '_BACKEND', '_DEFAULT_BACKEND', '_TF_DEPRECATION_WARNED', '_TRUTHY_JAX_X64_VALUES', '__all__', '__builtins__', '__cached__', '__doc__', '__file__', '__loader__', '__name__', '__package__', '__spec__', '_configure_jax_precision', '_initialize_backend', '_warn_tensorflow_deprecated', 'enum', 'get_backend', 'os', 'set_backend', 'warnings']

  enum
  os
  warnings

[CLASSES]

  CLASS: Backend
  SIGNATURE: (*values)
  DOCSTRING:
Create a collection of name/value pairs.

Example enumeration:

>>> class Color(Enum):
...     RED = 1
...     BLUE = 2
...     GREEN = 3

Access them by:

- attribute access:

  >>> Color.RED
  <Color.RED: 1>

- value lookup:

  >>> Color(1)
  <Color.RED: 1>

- name lookup:

  >>> Color['RED']
  <Color.RED: 1>

Enumerations can be iterated over, and know how many members they have:

>>> len(Color)
3

[FUNCTIONS]

  FUNCTION: _configure_jax_precision() -> None
  DOCSTRING: Configures JAX 64-bit precision based on the MERIDIAN_ENABLE_JAX_X64 env var.

  FUNCTION: _initialize_backend() -> meridian.backend.config.Backend
  DOCSTRING: Initializes the backend based on environment variables or defaults.

  FUNCTION: _warn_tensorflow_deprecated(stacklevel: int = 2) -> None
  DOCSTRING: Emits an idempotent deprecation warning when TensorFlow is enabled.

  FUNCTION: get_backend() -> meridian.backend.config.Backend
  DOCSTRING: Returns the current backend for Meridian.

  FUNCTION: set_backend(backend: Union[meridian.backend.config.Backend, str]) -> None
  DOCSTRING: Sets the backend for Meridian.

**Warning:** This function should ideally be called at the beginning of your
program, before any other Meridian modules are imported or used.

Changing the backend after Meridian's functions or classes have been
imported can lead to unpredictable behavior. This is because already-imported
modules will not reflect the backend change.

Changing the backend at runtime requires reloading the `meridian.backend`
module for the changes to take effect globally.

Args:
  backend: The backend to use, must be a member of the `Backend` enum or a
    valid string ('tensorflow', 'jax').

Raises:
  ValueError: If the provided backend is not valid.

  err_msg: The error message to be printed in case of failure.

  FUNCTION: normalize_tensor_protos(proto: google.protobuf.message.Message)
  DOCSTRING: Recursively normalizes TensorProto messages within a proto (In-place).

This ensures a consistent serialization format across different backends
(e.g., JAX vs TF) by repacking TensorProtos using the current backend's
canonical method (backend.make_tensor_proto). This handles differences
like using `bool_val` versus `tensor_content` for boolean tensors.

Args:
  proto: The protobuf message object to normalize. This object is modified in
    place.

[FUNCTIONS]

  FUNCTION: get_currency_symbol(currency_code: str | None = None, default: str = '$') -> str
  DOCSTRING: Resolves an ISO 4217 currency code into its display currency symbol.

Uses `babel.numbers.get_currency_symbol()` to resolve the display symbol for
the currency. If the code is None or empty, returns `default`. If the symbol
cannot be resolved, returns the uppercase currency code itself.

Args:
  currency_code: An optional ISO 4217 currency code (e.g., 'USD', 'EUR',
    'JPY').
  default: The fallback symbol if `currency_code` is None or empty. Defaults
    to `constants.DEFAULT_CURRENCY_SYMBOL` ('$').

Returns:
  The currency symbol string (e.g., '$', '€', '£', '¥').

  FUNCTION: is_valid_currency_code(currency_code: str | None) -> bool
  DOCSTRING: Returns True if the currency code is valid.

Args:
  currency_code: An optional ISO 4217 currency code.

Returns:
  True if the currency code is valid, False otherwise.

  FUNCTION: normalize_currency_code(currency_code: str | None) -> str | None
  DOCSTRING: Normalizes an ISO 4217 currency code to uppercase string, or None if invalid.

Args:
  currency_code: An optional ISO 4217 currency code.

Returns:
  The uppercase normalized ISO 4217 currency code if valid, or None.

[ALL ATTRIBUTES]
['Generic', 'OrderedListArgumentBuilder', 'Sequence', 'T', 'TypeVar', '__all__', '__builtins__', '__cached__', '__doc__', '__file__', '__loader__', '__name__', '__package__', '__spec__']

[SUBMODULES]

[CLASSES]

  CLASS: OrderedListArgumentBuilder
  SIGNATURE: (ordered_coords: collections.abc.Sequence[str])
  DOCSTRING:
A simple builder for an argument that expects an ordered list of values.

For example, some Meridian function requires a list/array of some values that:

- Must have the same length as some channel coordinates in the input data.
- Must be in the same order as some channel coordinates in the input data.

This argument builder can be bound to one such channel coordinates list, and
provides a user-friendly and human-readable way to seed it with user given
values.

For example, to create an array (list) of values that must be indexed on the
*paid media* channels in the input data:

```python
paid_media_channels_arg_builder = (
    OrderedListArgumentBuilder[float](input_data.get_all_paid_channels())
)
# Note: rather than creating this builder directly, use methods like
# `InputData.get_all_paid_channels_argument_builder()` where the container
# determines which coordinates to bind the builder with.

# Use `.with_default_value()` to set a default value for coordinates that are
# not given in `__call__`.
paid_media_channels_arg_builder = (
    paid_media_channels_arg_builder.with_default_value(0.3)
)

# Assuming we have paid media channels ['display', 'search', social'].
some_arg = paid_media_channels_arg_builder(
    display=0.1,
    social=0.25,
)
# some_arg == [0.1, 0.3, 0.25]
```

See: `InputData.get_paid_channels_argument_builder()`.
  METHODS:
    __call__(self, **kwargs) -> list[~T]
    __init__(self, ordered_coords: collections.abc.Sequence[str])
    with_default_value(self, default_value: ~T) -> 'OrderedListArgumentBuilder'

['DataValidationWarning', 'InputData', 'Sequence', '_FREQUENCY_BELOW_ONE_TOLERANCE', '_NUMPY_OBJECT_DTYPE', '__all__', '__builtins__', '__cached__', '__doc__', '__file__', '__loader__', '__name__', '__package__', '__spec__', '_aggregate_spend', '_check_dim_collection', '_check_dim_match', 'abc', 'arg_builder', 'backend', 'constants', 'currency', 'dataclasses', 'functools', 'np', 'tc', 'validator', 'warnings', 'xr']

[CLASSES]

  CLASS: DataValidationWarning
  DOCSTRING:
Invalid data that will raise ValueError in a future release.
  METHODS:

  CLASS: InputData
  SIGNATURE: (kpi: xarray.core.dataarray.DataArray, kpi_type: str, population: xarray.core.dataarray.DataArray, controls: xarray.core.dataarray.DataArray | None = None, revenue_per_kpi: xarray.core.dataarray.DataArray | None = None, media: xarray.core.dataarray.DataArray | None = None, media_spend: xarray.core.dataarray.DataArray | None = None, reach: xarray.core.dataarray.DataArray | None = None, frequency: xarray.core.dataarray.DataArray | None = None, rf_spend: xarray.core.dataarray.DataArray | None = None, organic_media: xarray.core.dataarray.DataArray | None = None, organic_reach: xarray.core.dataarray.DataArray | None = None, organic_frequency: xarray.core.dataarray.DataArray | None = None, non_media_treatments: xarray.core.dataarray.DataArray | None = None, currency_code: str | None = None) -> None
  DOCSTRING:
A data container for advertising data in a format supported by Meridian.

Attributes:
  kpi: A DataArray of dimensions `(n_geos, n_times)` containing the
    non-negative dependent variable. Typically this is the number of units
    sold, but it can be any metric, such as revenue or conversions.
  kpi_type: A string denoting whether the KPI is of a `'revenue'` or
    `'non-revenue'` type. When the `kpi_type` is `'non-revenue'` and
    `revenue_per_kpi` exists, ROI calibration is used and the analysis is run
    on revenue. When the `revenue_per_kpi` doesn't exist for the same
    `kpi_type`, custom ROI calibration is used and the analysis is run on KPI.
  population: A DataArray of dimensions `(n_geos,)` containing the population
    of each group. This variable is used to scale the KPI and media for
    modeling.
  controls: An optional DataArray of dimensions `(n_geos, n_times,
    n_controls)` containing control variable values.
  revenue_per_kpi: An optional DataArray of dimensions `(n_geos, n_times)`
    containing the average revenue amount per KPI unit. Although modeling is
    done on `kpi`, model analysis and optimization are done on `KPI *
    revenue_per_kpi` (revenue), if this value is available. If `kpi`
    corresponds to revenue, then an array of ones is passed automatically.
  media: An optional DataArray of dimensions `(n_geos, n_media_times,
    n_media_channels)` containing non-negative media execution values.
    Typically these are impressions, but it can be any metric, such as cost or
    clicks. `n_media_times` ≥ `n_times` is required, and the final `n_times`
    time periods must align with the time window of `kpi` and `controls`. Due
    to lagged effects, we recommend that the time window for media includes up
    to `max_lag` additional periods prior to this window. If `n_media_times` <
    `n_times` + `max_lag`, the model effectively imputes media history as zero
    (no media execution). If `n_media_times` > `n_times` + `max_lag`, then
    only the final `n_times` + `max_lag` periods are used to fit the model.
    `media` and `media_spend` must contain the same number of media channels
    in the same order. If either of these arguments is passed, then the other
    is not optional.
  media_spend: An optional `DataArray` containing the cost of each media
    channel. This is used as the denominator for ROI calculations. It is also
    used to calculate an assumed cost per media unit for post-modeling
    analysis such as response curves and budget optimization. Only the
    aggregate spend (across geos and time periods) is required for these
    calculations. However, a spend breakdown by geo and time period is
    required if `roi_calibration_period` is specified, and is recommended if
    conducting post-modeling analysis on a specific subset of geos and/or
    time periods. The DataArray shape can be `(n_geos, n_times,
    n_media_channels)` or `(n_media_channels,)` if the data is aggregated over
    `geo` and `time` dimensions. We recommend that the spend total aligns with
    the time window of the `kpi` and `controls` data, which is the time window
    over which incremental outcome of the ROI numerator is calculated.
    However, note that incremental outcome is influenced by media execution
    prior to this time window, through lagged effects, and excludes lagged
    effects beyond the time window of media executed during the time window.
    `media` and `media_spend` must contain the same number of media channels
    in the same order. If either of these arguments is passed, then the other
    is not optional. If a tensor of shape `(n_media_channels,)` is passed as
    `media_spend`, then it will be automatically allocated across geos and
    times proportionally to `media`, assuming a constant cost per media unit.
    Post-modeling analysis on a subset of geos and/or time periods is then
    based on this allocation, and a warning is issued.
  reach: An optional `DataArray` of dimensions `(n_geos, n_media_times,
    n_rf_channels)` containing non-negative `reach` values. It is required
    that `n_media_times` ≥ `n_times`, and the final `n_times` time periods
    must align with the time window of `kpi` and `controls`. The time window
    must include the time window of the `kpi` and `controls` data, but it is
    optional to include lagged time periods prior to the time window of the
    `kpi` and `controls` data. If lagged reach is not included, or if the
    lagged reach includes fewer than `max_lag` time periods, then the model
    calculates Adstock assuming that reach execution is zero prior to the
    first observed time period. We recommend including `n_times` + `max_lag`
    time periods, unless the value of `max_lag` is prohibitively large. If
    only `media` data is used, then `reach` will be `None`. `reach`,
    `frequency`, and `rf_spend` must contain the same number of media channels
    in the same order. If any of these arguments is passed, then the others
    are not optional.
  frequency: An optional `DataArray` of dimensions `(n_geos, n_media_times,
    n_rf_channels)` containing non-negative `frequency` values. It is required
    that `n_media_times` ≥ `n_times`, and the final `n_times` time periods
    must align with the time window of `kpi` and `controls`. The time window
    must include the time window of the `kpi` and `controls` data, but it is
    optional to include lagged time periods prior to the time window of the
    `kpi` and `controls` data. If lagged frequency is not included, or if the
    lagged frequency includes fewer than `max_lag` time periods, then the
    model calculates Adstock assuming that frequency execution is zero prior
    to the first observed time period. We recommend including `n_times` +
    `max_lag` time periods, unless the value of `max_lag` is prohibitively
    large. If only `media` data is used, then `frequency` will be `None`.
    `reach`, `frequency`, and `rf_spend` must contain the same number of media
    channels in the same order. If any of these arguments is passed, then the
    others are not optional.
  rf_spend: An optional `DataArray` containing the cost of each reach and
    frequency channel. This is used as the denominator for ROI calculations.
    It is also used to calculate an assumed cost per media unit for
    post-modeling analysis such as response curves and budget optimization.
    Only the aggregate spend (across geos and time periods) is required for
    these calculations. However, a spend breakdown by geo and time period is
    required if `rf_roi_calibration_period` is specified, and is recommended
    if conducting post-modeling analysis on a specific subset of geos and/or
    time periods. The DataArray shape can be `(n_rf_channels,)` or `(n_geos,
    n_times, n_rf_channels)`. The spend should be aggregated over geo and/or
    time dimensions that are not represented. We recommend that the spend
    total aligns with the time window of the `kpi` and `controls` data, which
    is the time window over which incremental outcome of the ROI numerator is
    calculated. However, note that incremental outcome is influenced by media
    execution prior to this time window, through lagged effects, and excludes
    lagged effects beyond the time window of media executed during the time
    window. If only `media` data is used, `rf_spend` will be `None`. `reach`,
    `frequency`, and `rf_spend` must contain the same number of media channels
    in the same order. If any of these arguments is passed, then the others
    are not optional. If a tensor of shape `(n_rf_channels,)` is passed as
    `rf_spend`, then it will be automatically allocated across geos and times
    proportionally to `(reach * frequency)`, assuming a constant cost per
    impression. Post-modeling analysis on a subset of geos and/or time periods
    is then based on this allocation, and a warning is issued.
  organic_media: An optional `DataArray` of dimensions `(n_geos,
    n_media_times, n_organic_media_channels)` containing non-negative organic
    media values. Organic media variables are media activities that have no
    direct cost. These may include impressions from newsletters, a blog post,
    social media activity or email campaigns but it can be any metric, such as
    clicks. `n_media_times` ≥ `n_times` is required, and the final `n_times`
    time periods must align with the time window of `kpi` and `controls`. Due
    to lagged effects, we recommend that the time window for organic media
    includes up to `max_lag` additional periods prior to this window. If
    `n_organic_media_times` < `n_times` + `max_lag`, the model effectively
    imputes organic media history. If `n_organic_media_times` > `n_times` +
    `max_lag`, then only the final `n_times` + `max_lag` periods are used to
    fit the model.
  organic_reach: An optional `DataArray` of dimensions `(n_geos,
    n_media_times, n_organic_rf_channels)` containing non-negative organic
    reach values. It is required that `n_media_times` ≥ `n_times`, and the
    final `n_times` time periods must align with the time window of `kpi` and
    `controls`. The time window must include the time window of the `kpi` and
    `controls` data, but it is optional to include lagged time periods prior
    to the time window of the `kpi` and `controls` data. If lagged reach is
    not included, or if the lagged reach includes fewer than `max_lag` time
    periods, then the model calculates Adstock assuming that reach execution
    is zero prior to the first observed time period. We recommend including
    `n_times` + `max_lag` time periods, unless the value of `max_lag` is
    prohibitively large. If no organic reach and frequency data is used, then
    `organic_reach` and `organic_frequency` will be `None`. `organic_reach`,
    and `organic_frequency` must contain the same number of channels in the
    same order. If any of these arguments is passed, then the other is not
    optional.
  organic_frequency: An optional `DataArray` of dimensions `(n_geos,
    n_media_times, n_organic_rf_channels)` containing non-negative organic
    frequency values. It is required that `n_media_times` ≥ `n_times`, and the
    final `n_times` time periods must align with the time window of `kpi` and
    `controls`. The time window must include the time window of the `kpi` and
    `controls` data, but it is optional to include lagged time periods prior
    to the time window of the `kpi` and `controls` data. If lagged frequency
    is not included, or if the lagged frequency includes fewer than `max_lag`
    time periods, then the model calculates Adstock assuming that frequency
    execution is zero prior to the first observed time period. We recommend
    including `n_times` + `max_lag` time periods, unless the value of
    `max_lag` is prohibitively large. If no organic reach and frequency data
    is used, then `organic_frequency` will be `None`. `organic_reach` and
    `organic_frequency` must contain the same number of channels in the same
    order. If any of these arguments is passed, then the other is not
    optional.
  non_media_treatments: An optional DataArray of dimensions `(n_geos, n_times,
    n_non_media_channels)` containing non-media treatment variables values.
    Non-media treatment variables are marketing activities taken by the
    advertiser not directly related to media. They have no direct marketing
    cost associated with them but unlike organic media variables there are no
    Adstock and Hill effects. They differ from control variables as they are
    considered to be intervenable and hence are treatment variables under the
    causal model. Some examples include running a promotion, the price of a
    product and a change in a product's packaging and/or design.
  currency_code: An optional ISO 4217 currency code string (e.g., 'USD',
    'EUR', 'JPY') indicating the currency denomination of all monetary values
    in this dataset (spend, revenue, budgets). If None, the currency is
    unspecified and reporting defaults to '$'. Default: None.
  METHODS:
    __eq__(self, other)
    __init__(self, kpi: xarray.core.dataarray.DataArray, kpi_type: str, population: xarray.core.dataarray.DataArray, controls: xarray.core.dataarray.DataArray | None = None, revenue_per_kpi: xarray.core.dataarray.DataArray | None = None, media: xarray.core.dataarray.DataArray | None = None, media_spend: xarray.core.dataarray.DataArray | None = None, reach: xarray.core.dataarray.DataArray | None = None, frequency: xarray.core.dataarray.DataArray | None = None, rf_spend: xarray.core.dataarray.DataArray | None = None, organic_media: xarray.core.dataarray.DataArray | None = None, organic_reach: xarray.core.dataarray.DataArray | None = None, organic_frequency: xarray.core.dataarray.DataArray | None = None, non_media_treatments: xarray.core.dataarray.DataArray | None = None, currency_code: str | None = None) -> None
    __post_init__(self)
    __replace__(self, /, **changes)
    __repr__(self)
    _allocate_spend(self, spend: xarray.core.dataarray.DataArray, media_units: xarray.core.dataarray.DataArray)
    _check_unique_names(self, dim: str, array: xarray.core.dataarray.DataArray | None)
    _coerce_object_arrays_to_float(self)

[FUNCTIONS]

  FUNCTION: _aggregate_spend(spend: xarray.core.dataarray.DataArray, calibration_period: numpy.ndarray | None) -> numpy.ndarray | None
  DOCSTRING: Aggregates spend for each channel over the calibration period.

Args:
  spend: An array with shape `(n_geos, n_times, n_channels)` to aggregate.
  calibration_period: An optional boolean array of shape `(n_media_times,
    n_channels)`. If provided, spend is filtered according to this period.

Returns:
  A 1-D array of aggregated media spend per channel, or `None` if `spend` is
  `None`.

  FUNCTION: _check_dim_collection(array: xarray.core.dataarray.DataArray | None, dims: collections.abc.Collection[collections.abc.Collection[str]])
  DOCSTRING: Checks if a DataArray has the correct collection of dimensions.

Arguments:
  array: A DataArray to be verified.
  dims: A collection of acceptable collections of dimensions. In case of the
    `media_spend` and `rf_spend` arrays, there are two acceptable collections
    of dimensions: `(channel)` and `(geo, time, channel)` where `channel` is
    either `'media_channel'` or `'rf_channel'`. In case of other arrays, there
    is only one acceptable collection of dimensions.

Raises:
  ValueError if the number of dimensions or dimension names are incorrect.

  FUNCTION: _check_dim_match(dim: str, arrays: collections.abc.Sequence[xarray.core.dataarray.DataArray | None])
  DOCSTRING: Verifies that the dimensions of the appropriate arrays match.

  CLASS: CoordToColumns
  SIGNATURE: (time: str = 'time', geo: str = 'geo', kpi: str = 'kpi', controls: collections.abc.Sequence[str] | None = None, revenue_per_kpi: str | None = None, population: str = 'population', media: collections.abc.Sequence[str] | None = None, media_spend: collections.abc.Sequence[str] | None = None, reach: collections.abc.Sequence[str] | None = None, frequency: collections.abc.Sequence[str] | None = None, rf_spend: collections.abc.Sequence[str] | None = None, non_media_treatments: collections.abc.Sequence[str] | None = None, organic_media: collections.abc.Sequence[str] | None = None, organic_reach: collections.abc.Sequence[str] | None = None, organic_frequency: collections.abc.Sequence[str] | None = None) -> None
  DOCSTRING:
A mapping between the desired and actual column names in the input data.

    values in the input data.
