  CLASS: RandomHoldoutSpec
  SIGNATURE: (ratio: float, seed: int | None = None, *, balanced: bool = False) -> None
  DOCSTRING:
Random holdout specification.

This specification is *input only*: it declares the intent to hold out a
random subset of observations, not the subset itself. The framework draws the
random sample exactly once, when the model is compiled against its input
data, producing a holdout mask over `(n_geos, n_times)` that becomes part of
the model's state. All downstream computation reads that resolved holdout,
never this specification.

The draw is stratified by geo: every geo holds out the same number of time
periods, `round(ratio * n_times)`, sampled independently per geo by default.
With `balanced=True` the draw is additionally balanced across time periods,
so that the number of held-out observations per time period differs by at
most one. The per-time counts can differ by one because the total number of
held-out observations is not necessarily a multiple of `n_times`.

Both this specification and the resolved draw are serialized: the former as
provenance recording how the holdout was requested, the latter as the
authoritative record of what was actually held out. The resolved draw is
stored declaratively in `HoldoutSpec.resolved`, as the geos and date ranges
that were selected, rather than as a raw mask. On deserialization it always
takes precedence and is never re-drawn, because `seed` alone does not
reproduce a draw across computational backends (TensorFlow vs. JAX),
library versions, or different input data row/coordinate orderings (since the
PRNG stream is consumed in the order geos appear in `input_data.geo`).

If a serialized model carries only this specification and no resolved draw,
the draw is performed on load and a warning is emitted. The resulting holdout
will not match the one used during the original fit. Such a model remains
valid for inference, since the holdout does not participate in inference, but
its train/test predictive accuracy metrics are not meaningful.

Attributes:
  ratio: The fraction of observations to hold out. Must be strictly between
    0.0 and 1.0.
  seed: Optional random seed for deterministic holdout generation.
  balanced: Whether to balance the draw across time periods as well as geos,
    so that per-time holdout counts differ by at most one. When `False`, each
    geo's held-out periods are a simple random sample, independent of the
    other geos. Default: `False`.
  METHODS:
    __delattr__(self, name)
    __eq__(self, other)
    __hash__(self)
    __init__(self, ratio: float, seed: int | None = None, *, balanced: bool = False) -> None
    __post_init__(self) -> None
    __replace__(self, /, **changes)
    __repr__(self)
    __setattr__(self, name, value)

  CLASS: SaturationType
  SIGNATURE: (*values)
  DOCSTRING:
Create a collection of name/value pairs.

Example enumeration:

>>> class Color(Enum):
...     RED = 1
...     BLUE = 2
...     GREEN = 3

Access them by:

- attribute access:

  >>> Color.RED
  <Color.RED: 1>

- value lookup:

  >>> Color(1)
  <Color.RED: 1>

- name lookup:

  >>> Color['RED']
  <Color.RED: 1>

Enumerations can be iterated over, and know how many members they have:

>>> len(Color)
3

>>> list(Color)
[<Color.RED: 1>, <Color.BLUE: 2>, <Color.GREEN: 3>]

Methods can be added to enumerations, and members can have their own
attributes -- see the documentation for details.
  METHODS:

  CLASS: _ModelSpecWithChangepoints
  SIGNATURE: (prior: meridian.model.prior_distribution.PriorDistribution = <factory>, media_effects_dist: str = 'log_normal', hill_before_adstock: bool = False, max_lag: int = 8, unique_sigma_for_each_geo: bool = False, media_prior_type: str | None = None, rf_prior_type: str | None = None, paid_media_prior_type: str | None = None, roi_calibration: meridian.model.spec.CalibrationSpec | None = None, roi_calibration_period: numpy.ndarray | None = None, rf_roi_calibration: meridian.model.spec.CalibrationSpec | None = None, rf_roi_calibration_period: numpy.ndarray | None = None, organic_media_prior_type: str = 'contribution', organic_rf_prior_type: str = 'contribution', non_media_treatments_prior_type: str = 'contribution', non_media_baseline_values: Union[collections.abc.Mapping[str, float | str], Sequence[float | str], NoneType] = None, knots: int | collections.abc.Collection[int] | None = None, baseline_geo: int | str | None = None, holdout: meridian.model.spec.HoldoutSpec | None = None, holdout_id: numpy.ndarray | None = None, population_scaled_controls: Optional[Sequence[str]] = None, control_population_scaling_id: numpy.ndarray | None = None, population_scaled_non_media_channels: Optional[Sequence[str]] = None, non_media_population_scaling_id: numpy.ndarray | None = None, adstock_decay_spec: str | collections.abc.Mapping[str, str] = 'geometric', saturation_spec: str | collections.abc.Mapping[str, str] = 'hill', enable_aks: bool = False, allows_negative_aggregate_baseline: bool = True, changepoints: collections.abc.Mapping[str, collections.abc.Collection[str | datetime.datetime | datetime.date | numpy.datetime64 | None]] | None = None, changepoint_media_effects_dist: str = 'log_normal', changepoint_media_prior_type: str = 'roi', changepoint_rf_prior_type: str = 'roi') -> None
  DOCSTRING:
A `ModelSpec` with time-varying media effects. Internal only.

`WeeklyOptimizationGrid` and `ReachFrequencyOptimizationProcessor` raise
`NotImplementedError` for a model with changepoints.

Attributes:
  changepoints: Maps a paid media or reach and frequency channel name to the
    dates where its effect may change. Each date must be a time coordinate of
    the input data other than the first, and starts a new interval. For
    example, `{'TV': ['2024-12-30']}` gives TV one effect before 2024-12-30
    and another from then on. Channels that aren't listed keep one effect for
    all time periods. Dates can be `datetime.date`, `datetime.datetime`,
    `np.datetime64` or 'YYYY-MM-DD' strings, and are stored as tuples of
    `datetime.date`. Default: `None`, which means no time-varying effects.
  changepoint_media_effects_dist: The distribution of the media random effects
    of the paid media and RF channels in `changepoints`, either `'log_normal'`
    or `'normal'`. Other channels use `media_effects_dist`. Unlike
    `media_effects_dist`, it also applies to national models. Default:
    `'log_normal'`.
  changepoint_media_prior_type: The prior type of the paid media channels in
    `changepoints`: `'roi'`, `'mroi'`, `'contribution'` or `'coefficient'`.
    Other paid media channels use `media_prior_type`. Default: `'roi'`.
  changepoint_rf_prior_type: The prior type of the RF channels in
    `changepoints`: `'roi'`, `'mroi'`, `'contribution'` or `'coefficient'`.
    Other RF channels use `rf_prior_type`. Default: `'roi'`.
  METHODS:
    __delattr__(self, name)

  CLASS: CenteringAndScalingTransformer
  SIGNATURE: (tensor: jax.Array, population: jax.Array, population_scaling_id: jax.Array | None = None)
  DOCSTRING:
Applies centering and scaling transformations to a tensor.

This class transforms a tensor so each variable has mean zero and standard
deviation one. Optionally, each variable can be scaled by population before
the centering and scaling transformations are applied. The class stores the
mean and standard deviation of each variable.
  METHODS:
    __init__(self, tensor: jax.Array, population: jax.Array, population_scaling_id: jax.Array | None = None)

  CLASS: KpiTransformer
  SIGNATURE: (kpi: jax.Array, population: jax.Array)
  DOCSTRING:
Contains forward and inverse KPI transformation methods.

This class stores coefficients to scale KPI. It first divides KPI by the
population of each geo, and then centers and scales the result by the mean
and standard deviation of the population-scaled KPI across all geos and time
periods.
  METHODS:
    __init__(self, kpi: jax.Array, population: jax.Array)

  CLASS: MediaTransformer
  SIGNATURE: (media: jax.Array, population: jax.Array)
  DOCSTRING:
Contains forward and inverse media transformation methods.

This class stores scale factors computed from per-geo medians of the `media`
data, normalized by the geo population.
  METHODS:
    __init__(self, media: jax.Array, population: jax.Array)

  CLASS: BudgetOptimizationSpec
  SIGNATURE: (start_date: datetime.date | None = None, end_date: datetime.date | None = None, date_interval_tag: str = '', *, optimization_name: str, grid_name: str, group_id: str | None = None, confidence_level: float = 0.9, scenario: meridian.analysis.optimizer.FixedBudgetScenario | meridian.analysis.optimizer.FlexibleBudgetScenario = <factory>, constraints: collections.abc.Sequence[meridian.schema.processors.budget_optimization_processor.ChannelConstraintAbs | meridian.schema.processors.budget_optimization_processor.ChannelConstraintRel] = <factory>, kpi_type: meridian.schema.processors.common.KpiType = <KpiType.REVENUE: 2>, grid: meridian.analysis.optimizer.OptimizationGrid | None = None, include_response_curves: bool = True, new_data: meridian.analysis.tensors.DataTensors | None = None, use_optimal_frequency: bool = True, max_frequency: float | None = None, batch_size: int = 100) -> None
  DOCSTRING:
Spec dataclass for marketing budget optimization processor.

This spec is used both as user input to inform the budget optimization
processor of its constraints and parameters, as well as an output structure
that is serializable to a `BudgetOptimizationSpec` proto. The latter serves
as a metadata embedded in a `BudgetOptimizationResult`.

Attributes:
  objective: Always defined as KPI.
  scenario: The optimization scenario (whether fixed or flexible).
  constraints: Per-channel budget constraints. Defaults to relative
    constraints `[1, 2]` for spend_constraint_lower and spend_constraint_upper
    if not specified.
  kpi_type: A `common.KpiType` enum denoting whether the optimized KPI is of a
    `'revenue'` or `'non-revenue'` type.
  grid: The optimization grid to use for the optimization. If None, a new grid
    will be created within the optimizer.
  include_response_curves: Whether to include response curves in the output.
    Setting this to `False` improves performance if only optimization result
    is needed.
  new_data: The new data to use for the optimization. If None, the training
    data will be used.
  use_optimal_frequency: Whether to use the optimal frequency. If set to
    `False`, `max_frequency` is ignored.
  max_frequency: The max frequency to use for the optimal frequency search
    space. If not set when `use_optimal_frequency` is set to `True`, the max
    frequency of the input data is used.
  batch_size: Maximum draws per chain in each batch. The calculation is run in
    batches to avoid memory exhaustion. If a memory error occurs, try reducing
    `batch_size`. The calculation will generally be faster with larger
    `batch_size` values. Default is 100.
  METHODS:
    __delattr__(self, name)
    __eq__(self, other)
    __hash__(self)
    __init__(self, start_date: datetime.date | None = None, end_date: datetime.date | None = None, date_interval_tag: str = '', *, optimization_name: str, grid_name: str, group_id: str | None = None, confidence_level: float = 0.9, scenario: meridian.analysis.optimizer.FixedBudgetScenario | meridian.analysis.optimizer.FlexibleBudgetScenario = <factory>, constraints: collections.abc.Sequence[meridian.schema.processors.budget_optimization_processor.ChannelConstraintAbs | meridian.schema.processors.budget_optimization_processor.ChannelConstraintRel] = <factory>, kpi_type: meridian.schema.processors.common.KpiType = <KpiType.REVENUE: 2>, grid: meridian.analysis.optimizer.OptimizationGrid | None = None, include_response_curves: bool = True, new_data: meridian.analysis.tensors.DataTensors | None = None, use_optimal_frequency: bool = True, max_frequency: float | None = None, batch_size: int = 100) -> None
    __post_init__(self)
    __replace__(self, /, **changes)
    __repr__(self)
    __setattr__(self, name, value)
    resolver(self, time_coordinates: meridian.data.time_coordinates.TimeCoordinates) -> 'DatedSpecResolver'
    to_proto(self) -> mmm.v1.marketing.optimization.budget_optimization_pb2.BudgetOptimizationSpec
    validate(self)

  CLASS: ChannelConstraintAbs
  SIGNATURE: (channel_name: str, abs_lowerbound: float, abs_upperbound: float) -> None
  DOCSTRING:
A budget constraint on a channel.

Constraint attributes in this dataclass are absolute values. Useful to
represent resolved absolute constraint values in an output spec metadata.

Attributes:

  abs_lowerbound: A simple absolute lower bound value for a channel's spend.

  CLASS: ChannelConstraintRel
  SIGNATURE: (channel_name: str, spend_constraint_lower: float, spend_constraint_upper: float) -> None
  DOCSTRING:
A budget constraint on a channel.

Constraint attributes in this dataclass are relative ratios. Useful for user
input spec.

Attributes:
  channel_name: The name of the channel.
  spend_constraint_lower: The spend constraint lower of a channel is the
    change in ratio w.r.t. the channel's historical spend. The absolute lower
    bound value is equal to `(1 - spend_constraint_lower) *
    hist_channel_spend)`. The value must be between `[0, 1]`.
  spend_constraint_upper: The spend constraint upper of a channel is the
    change in ratio w.r.t. the channel's historical spend. The absolute upper
    bound value is equal to `(1 + spend_constraint_upper) *
    hist_channel_spend)`. The value must be non-negative.
  METHODS:
    __delattr__(self, name)
    __eq__(self, other)
    __hash__(self)
    __init__(self, channel_name: str, spend_constraint_lower: float, spend_constraint_upper: float) -> None
    __post_init__(self)
    __replace__(self, /, **changes)
    __repr__(self)
    __setattr__(self, name, value)

  CLASS: TargetMetric
  SIGNATURE: (*values)
  DOCSTRING:
Create a collection of name/value pairs.

Example enumeration:

>>> class Color(Enum):
...     RED = 1
...     BLUE = 2
...     GREEN = 3

Access them by:

- attribute access:

  >>> Color.RED
  <Color.RED: 1>

- value lookup:

  >>> Color(1)
  <Color.RED: 1>

- name lookup:

  >>> Color['RED']
  <Color.RED: 1>

Enumerations can be iterated over, and know how many members they have:

>>> len(Color)
3

>>> list(Color)
[<Color.RED: 1>, <Color.BLUE: 2>, <Color.GREEN: 3>]

Methods can be added to enumerations, and members can have their own
attributes -- see the documentation for details.
  METHODS:

  new_data: Optional `DataTensors` container with optional tensors: `media`,
    `reach`, `frequency`, `organic_media`, `organic_reach`,
    `organic_frequency`, `non_media_treatments` and `revenue_per_kpi`. If
    `None`, the incremental outcome is calculated using the `InputData`
    provided to the Meridian object. If `new_data` is provided, the
    incremental outcome is calculated using the new tensors in `new_data` and
    the original values of the remaining tensors. For example,
    `incremental_outcome(new_data=DataTensors(media=new_media)` computes the
    incremental outcome using `new_media` and the original values of `reach`,
    `frequency`, `organic_media`, `organic_reach`, `organic_frequency`,
    `non_media_treatments` and `revenue_per_kpi`. If any of the tensors in
    `new_data` is provided with a different number of time periods than in
    `InputData`, then all tensors must be provided with the same number of
    time periods, and `new_data.time` must be provided.
  media_selected_times: Optional list of date strings specifying a subset of
    time periods in `new_data`, if provided, or from model's original media
    data.
  include_non_paid_channels: Boolean. If `True`, the incremental outcome
    includes non-paid channels. Defaults to `False`.
  METHODS:
    __delattr__(self, name)
    __eq__(self, other)
    __hash__(self)
    __init__(self, *, aggregate_times: bool = True, new_data: meridian.analysis.tensors.DataTensors | None = None, media_selected_times: collections.abc.Sequence[str] | None = None, include_non_paid_channels: bool = False) -> None
    __post_init__(self)
    __replace__(self, /, **changes)
    __repr__(self)
    __setattr__(self, name, value)
    validate(self)

  CLASS: MediaSummarySpec
  SIGNATURE: (*, aggregate_times: bool = True, marginal_roi_by_reach: bool = True, include_non_paid_channels: bool = False, new_data: meridian.analysis.tensors.DataTensors | None = None, media_selected_times: collections.abc.Sequence[str] | None = None) -> None
  DOCSTRING:
Stores parameters needed for creating media summary metrics.

Attributes:
  aggregate_times: Boolean. If `True`, the media summary metrics are
    aggregated over time. Defaults to `True`.
  marginal_roi_by_reach: Boolean. Marginal ROI (mROI) is defined as the return
    on the next dollar spent. If this argument is `True`, the assumption is
    that the next dollar spent only impacts reach, holding frequency constant.
    If this argument is `False`, the assumption is that the next dollar spent
    only impacts frequency, holding reach constant. Defaults to `True`.
  include_non_paid_channels: Boolean. If `True`, the media summary metrics
    include non-paid channels. Defaults to `False`.
  new_data: Optional `DataTensors` container with optional tensors: `media`,
    `reach`, `frequency`, `organic_media`, `organic_reach`,
    `organic_frequency`, `non_media_treatments` and `revenue_per_kpi`. If
    `None`, the metrics are calculated using the `InputData` provided to the
    Meridian object. If `new_data` is provided, the metrics are calculated
    using the new tensors in `new_data` and the original values of the
    remaining tensors.
  media_selected_times: Optional list of date strings specifying a subset of
    time periods in `new_data`, if provided, or from model's original media
    data.
  METHODS:

  CLASS: ReachFrequencyOptimizationProcessor
  SIGNATURE: (trained_model: meridian.model.model.Meridian | meridian.schema.processors.model_processor.TrainedModel)
  DOCSTRING:
A Processor for marketing reach and frequency optimization.
  METHODS:
    __call__(self, specs: collections.abc.Sequence[~S], output: mmm.v1.mmm_pb2.Mmm)
    __init__(self, trained_model: meridian.model.model.Meridian | meridian.schema.processors.model_processor.TrainedModel)

    _to_reach_frequency_optimization_result(self, spec: meridian.schema.processors.reach_frequency_optimization_processor.ReachFrequencyOptimizationSpec, optimal_frequency: xarray.core.dataset.Dataset, response_curve: xarray.core.dataset.Dataset, spend_data: xarray.core.dataset.Dataset) -> mmm.v1.marketing.optimization.reach_frequency_optimization_pb2.ReachFrequencyOptimizationResult
    _to_target_precision(self, value: float) -> float
    execute(self, specs: collections.abc.Sequence[meridian.schema.processors.reach_frequency_optimization_processor.ReachFrequencyOptimizationSpec]) -> mmm.v1.marketing.optimization.reach_frequency_optimization_pb2.ReachFrequencyOptimization

  CLASS: ReachFrequencyOptimizationSpec
  SIGNATURE: (start_date: datetime.date | None = None, end_date: datetime.date | None = None, date_interval_tag: str = '', *, optimization_name: str, grid_name: str, group_id: str | None = None, confidence_level: float = 0.9, min_frequency: float = 1.0, max_frequency: float | None = None, rf_channels: collections.abc.Sequence[str] = <factory>, kpi_type: meridian.schema.processors.common.KpiType = <KpiType.REVENUE: 2>) -> None
  DOCSTRING:
Spec dataclass for marketing reach and frequency optimization processor.

A frequency grid is generated using the range `[rounded_min_frequency,
rounded_max_frequency]` and a step size of `STEP_SIZE=0.1`.
`rounded_min_frequency` and `rounded_max_frequency` are rounded to the
nearest multiple of `STEP_SIZE`.

This spec is used both as user input to inform the R&F optimization processor
of its constraints and parameters, as well as an output structure that is
serializable to a `ReachFrequencyOptimizationSpec` proto. The latter serves as
a metadata embedded in a `ReachFrequencyOptimizationResult`. The output spec
in the proto reflects the actual numbers used to generate the reach and
frequency optimization result.

Attributes:
  min_frequency: The minimum frequency constraint for each channel. Must be
    greater than or equal to `1.0`. Defaults to `1.0`.
  max_frequency: The maximum frequency constraint for each channel. Must be
    greater than min_frequency. Defaults to None. If this value is set to
    None, the model's max frequency will be used.
  rf_channels: The R&F media channels in the model. When resolved with a
    model, the model's R&F channels will be present here. Ignored when used as
    input.

  FUNCTION: _is_bool_parameter(param_name: 'str', dist_class: 'type[backend.tfd.Distribution | backend.bijectors.Bijector]') -> 'bool'
  DOCSTRING: Returns whether `param_name` is a boolean constructor parameter of `dist_class`.

A parameter is considered boolean if it is one of the well-known boolean
parameters in `_KNOWN_BOOL_PARAMETERS` or if its default value in the
`__init__` signature of `dist_class` is a `bool` (e.g.
`force_probs_to_zero_outside_support`). Integer parameters such as
`BatchBroadcast.with_shape` are not boolean.

Args:
  param_name: The name of the constructor parameter.
  dist_class: The distribution or bijector class being deserialized.

Returns:
  True if `param_name` is a boolean constructor parameter of `dist_class`.

[CLASSES]

  CLASS: HyperparametersSerde
  SIGNATURE: ()
  DOCSTRING:
Serializes and deserializes a ModelSpec into a `Hyperparameters` proto.

Note that this Serde only handles the Hyperparameters part of ModelSpec.
The 'prior' attribute of ModelSpec is serialized/deserialized separately
using DistributionSerde.

Several `ModelSpec` attributes come in pairs: a declarative attribute and the
deprecated array it supersedes. This Serde is a faithful mirror of whichever
of them are set, and implements no precedence between them: it writes the
declarative proto field if and only if the declarative attribute is set, and
the deprecated proto field if and only if the deprecated attribute is set.
Deserialization is the exact inverse, so every `ModelSpec` state round-trips
unchanged -- including the state where both are set, which `ModelSpec` allows
with a warning.

Resolving which of a pair governs is `ModelContext`'s job, not this one. Were
this Serde to make that decision too, the two could drift apart, and a model
could silently fit differently after a save and reload.
  METHODS:
    deserialize(self, serialized: mmm.v1.model.meridian.meridian_model_pb2.Hyperparameters, serialized_version: str = '', *, input_data: meridian.data.input_data.InputData | None = None) -> meridian.model.spec.ModelSpec
    serialize(self, obj: meridian.model.spec.ModelSpec, *, model_context: meridian.model.context.ModelContext | None = None) -> mmm.v1.model.meridian.meridian_model_pb2.Hyperparameters

[FUNCTIONS]

  FUNCTION: _deserialize_calibration(config: mmm.v1.model.meridian.meridian_model_pb2.CalibrationConfig, dates: collections.abc.Sequence[datetime.date], *, spec_name: str) -> meridian.model.spec.CalibrationSpec | None
  DOCSTRING: Converts a `CalibrationConfig` proto into a `CalibrationSpec`.

  FUNCTION: _deserialize_changepoints(serialized: mmm.v1.model.meridian.meridian_model_pb2.Hyperparameters) -> dict[str, typing.Any]
  DOCSTRING: Reads the time-varying media effects fields of `serialized`.

Args:
  serialized: The `Hyperparameters` proto to read.

Returns:
  The arguments of `spec._ModelSpecWithChangepoints` that `serialized` sets.
  Empty if it sets none, which means a model without time-varying effects.

  FUNCTION: _deserialize_date_range(interval: mmm.v1.common.date_interval_pb2.DateInterval, dates: collections.abc.Sequence[datetime.date], *, spec_name: str) -> meridian.model.spec.DateRange
  DOCSTRING: Converts a half-open `DateInterval` proto into a closed `DateRange`.

  FUNCTION: _serialize_changepoints(obj: meridian.model.spec.ModelSpec, proto: mmm.v1.model.meridian.meridian_model_pb2.Hyperparameters) -> None
  DOCSTRING: Writes the time-varying media effects fields of `obj` into `proto`.

They are written only when `obj` has changepoints, so a model without them
saves exactly as before. The three optional fields are always written for a
model with changepoints, so it loads with the same settings even if their
defaults change.

  proto: The proto to write into.

  FUNCTION: _serialize_date_range(date_range: meridian.model.spec.DateRange, period_ends: collections.abc.Mapping[datetime.date, datetime.date], *, spec_name: str) -> mmm.v1.common.date_interval_pb2.DateInterval
  DOCSTRING: Converts a closed `DateRange` into a half-open `DateInterval` proto.

An omitted bound is left unset rather than resolved against the data, so that
an open-ended range survives a round trip as an open-ended range.

Args:
  date_range: The closed date range to convert.
  period_ends: Exclusive period end for each time coordinate.
  spec_name: The `ModelSpec` attribute being serialized, used in error
    messages.

Returns:
  The equivalent half-open `DateInterval`.

Raises:
  ValueError: If `end_date` is not one of the time coordinates, and so has no
    period whose end could terminate the interval.

  DOCSTRING: Converts a `GeoHoldoutSpec` into a `GeoDateRangeHoldout` proto.

  FUNCTION: _serialize_holdout(holdout: meridian.model.spec.HoldoutSpec, period_ends: collections.abc.Mapping[datetime.date, datetime.date], *, resolved: collections.abc.Sequence[meridian.model.spec.GeoHoldoutSpec] | None) -> mmm.v1.model.meridian.meridian_model_pb2.HoldoutConfig
  DOCSTRING: Converts a `HoldoutSpec` into a `HoldoutConfig` proto.

  FUNCTION: _warn_if_random_holdout_is_unresolved(holdout: meridian.model.spec.HoldoutSpec | None, holdout_id: numpy.ndarray | None) -> None

[FUNCTIONS]

  FUNCTION: _get_equally_spaced_knot_locations(n_times, n_knots)
  DOCSTRING: Equally spaced knot locations starting at the endpoints.

  FUNCTION: get_knot_info(n_times: int, knots: int | collections.abc.Collection[int] | None, enable_aks: bool = False, data: meridian.data.input_data.InputData | None = None, is_national: bool = False) -> meridian.schema.serde.legacy_aks_v1_7_1.KnotInfo
  DOCSTRING: Returns the number of knots, knot locations, and weights.

Args:
  n_times: The number of time periods in the data.
  knots: An optional integer or a collection of integers indicating the knots
    used to estimate time effects. When `knots` is a collection of integers,
    the knot locations are provided by that collection. Zero corresponds to a
    knot at the first time period, one corresponds to a knot at the second
    time, ..., and `(n_times - 1)` corresponds to a knot at the last time
    period. When `knots` is an integer, then there are knots with locations
    equally spaced across the time periods (including knots at zero and
    `(n_times - 1)`. When `knots` is `1`, there is a single common regression
    coefficient used for all time periods. If `knots` is `None`, then the
    numbers of knots used is equal to the number of time periods. This is
    equivalent to each time period having its own regression coefficient.
  enable_aks: A boolean indicating whether to use the Automatic Knot Selection
    algorithm to select optimal number of knots for running the model instead
    of the default 1 for national and n_times for non-national models.
  data: An Optional InputData object used by the Automatic Knot Selection
    algorithm to calculate optimal number of knots from the provided Input
    Data.
  is_national: A boolean indicator whether to adapt the knot information for a
    national model.

Returns:
  A KnotInfo that contains the number of knots, the location of knots, and the
  weights used to multiply with the knot values to get time-varying
  coefficients.

  FUNCTION: get_legacy_knots(data: meridian.data.input_data.InputData, is_national: bool) -> list[int]
  DOCSTRING: Run the legacy AKS algorithm against the provided data.

[FUNCTIONS]

  FUNCTION: _get_schema_version() -> str
  DOCSTRING: Returns the current `mmm-proto-schema` version string.

  FUNCTION: _prior_to_serialize(model_spec: meridian.model.spec.ModelSpec) -> meridian.model.prior_distribution.PriorDistribution
  DOCSTRING: Returns the prior to save for a model with `model_spec`.

For a model with changepoints, it includes the priors of time-varying media
effects, even if they are the defaults, so that the model loads with the same
priors. For a model without changepoints, it never includes them: they don't
affect the model, and older versions of Meridian can't load them.

Args:
  model_spec: The model spec of the model being saved.

Returns:
  `model_spec.prior`, as a `_PriorDistributionWithChangepoints` if the model
  has changepoints, and as a `PriorDistribution` otherwise.

  FUNCTION: load_meridian(file_path: str, distribution_function_registry: meridian.schema.serde.function_registry.FunctionRegistry | None = None, eda_function_registry: meridian.schema.serde.function_registry.FunctionRegistry | None = None, force_deserialization: bool = False) -> meridian.model.model.Meridian
  DOCSTRING: Load the model object from an `MmmKernel` proto file path.

Supported file types:
  - `binpb` (wire-format proto)
  - `txtpb` (text-format proto)
  - `textproto` (text-format proto)

Args:
  file_path: File path to load a serialized model object from.
  distribution_function_registry: A lookup table that maps string keys to
    custom functions to be used as parameters in various `tfp.distributions`.
  eda_function_registry: A lookup table that maps string keys to custom
    functions to be used in `EDASpec`.
  force_deserialization: If True, bypasses the `schema_version` compatibility
    check (emitting a warning instead of raising a `ValueError` when the
    serialized model has a newer or unparseable `mmm-proto-schema` version) as
    well as the safety check that validates whether functions within a
    function registry have changed after serialization. Use with caution. This
    should only be used if you have intentionally modified a custom function
    or are loading a newer schema version and are confident that the changes
    will not affect the deserialized model. A safer alternative for function
    changes is to first deserialize the model with the original functions and
    then serialize it with the new ones.

Returns:
  Model object loaded from the file path.

  FUNCTION: save_meridian(mmm: meridian.model.model.Meridian, file_path: str, distribution_function_registry: meridian.schema.serde.function_registry.FunctionRegistry | None = None, eda_function_registry: meridian.schema.serde.function_registry.FunctionRegistry | None = None)
  DOCSTRING: Save the model object as an `MmmKernel` proto in the given filepath.

Supported file types:
  - `binpb` (wire-format proto)
  - `txtpb` (text-format proto)
  - `textproto` (text-format proto)

Args:
  mmm: Model object to save.
  file_path: File path to save a serialized model object. If the file name
    ends with `.binpb`, it will be saved in the wire-format. If the filename
    ends with `.txtpb` or `.textproto`, it will be saved in the text-format.
  distribution_function_registry: Optional. A lookup table that maps string
    keys to custom functions to be used as parameters in various
    `tfp.distributions`.
  eda_function_registry: A lookup table that maps string keys to custom
    functions to be used in `EDASpec`.

  FUNCTION: make_sample_dataset(n_chains: int, n_draws: int, n_geos: int = 5, n_controls: int = 2, n_knots: int = 0, n_times: int = 0, n_media_channels: int = 0, n_rf_channels: int = 0, n_organic_media_channels: int = 0, n_organic_rf_channels: int = 0, n_non_media_channels: int = 0) -> xarray.core.dataset.Dataset
  DOCSTRING: Creates a sample dataset with all relevant Meridian dimensions.

Args:
  n_chains: The number of chains.
  n_draws: The number of draws per chain.
  n_geos: The number of geos.
  n_controls: The number of control variables.
  n_knots: The number of knots.
  n_times: The number of time periods.
  n_media_channels: The number of media channels.
  n_rf_channels: The number of reach and frequency channels.
  n_organic_media_channels: The number of organic media channels.
  n_organic_rf_channels: The number of organic reach and frequency channels.
  n_non_media_channels: The number of non-media channels.

Returns:
  An xarray Dataset with sample data.

  FUNCTION: convert_times_to_date_intervals(times: collections.abc.Sequence[str] | collections.abc.Sequence[datetime.date] | pandas.core.indexes.datetimes.DatetimeIndex) -> collections.abc.Mapping[str, mmm.v1.common.date_interval_pb2.DateInterval]
  DOCSTRING: Creates a date interval for each time in `times` as dict values.

Args:
  times: Sequence of date strings in YYYY-MM-DD format, datetime.date objects,
    or a pandas DatetimeIndex.

Returns:
  Mapping that maps each time in `times` (string form) to the corresponding
  date interval.

Raises:
  ValueError: If `times` has fewer than 2 elements or if the time coordinates
    are not regularly spaced.

  FUNCTION: create_date_interval_pb(start_date: datetime.date, end_date: datetime.date, tag: str = '') -> mmm.v1.common.date_interval_pb2.DateInterval
  DOCSTRING: Creates a `DateInterval` proto for the given start and end dates.

Args:
  start_date: A datetime object representing the start date.
  end_date: A datetime object representing the end date.
  tag: An optional tag to identify the date interval.

Returns:
  Returns a date interval proto wrapping the start/end dates.

  FUNCTION: dates_from_date_interval_proto(date_interval: mmm.v1.common.date_interval_pb2.DateInterval) -> tuple[datetime.date, datetime.date]
  DOCSTRING: Returns a tuple of `[start, end)` date range from a `DateInterval` proto.

  FUNCTION: from_date_proto(date_proto: google.type.date_pb2.Date) -> datetime.date
  DOCSTRING: Converts a `google.type.Date` proto into a `datetime.date`.
